Which one is more likely? Let's search memory of `extract_tables` from pgcli. I can try to recall from actual source code. I found a mental image of `parseutils.py`:
```
def extract_tables(sql):
    """Extract table names from a SQL statement."""
    tables = []
    for statement in sqlparse.parse(sql):
        for token in statement.tokens:
            if isinstance(token, Identifier):
                tables.append((token.get_schema(), token.get_real_name(), token.get_alias()))
            elif isinstance(token, IdentifierList):
                for identifier in token.get_identifiers():
                    tables.append((identifier.get_schema(), identifier.get_real_name(), identifier.get_alias()))
    return tables
```
This returns tuples. Then in `sqlcompletion.py`, they might use:
```
tables = [{'schema': t[0], 'name': t[1], 'alias': t[2]} for t in extract_tables(full_text)]
```
This would always include alias. So output with alias None.

Let's search memory of `sqlcompletion.py` for `extract_tables` usage. I can visualize:
```
def suggest_based_on_last_token(last_token, text_before_cursor, full_text, identifier):
    if not last_token:
        return [{'type': 'keyword'}]
    elif last_token.value.lower() == 'select':
        return [{'type': 'column', 'tables': extract_tables(full_text)},
                {'type': 'function', 'schema': []}]
```
If `extract_tables` returns tuples, then `tables` is list of tuples. But then `PGCompleter` would need to handle tuples. Let's search memory of `PGCompleter.get_completions`:
```
if suggestion['type'] == 'column':
    tables = suggestion['tables']
    for table in tables:
        schema, name, alias = table
        ...
```
This is possible. But I recall seeing `table['schema']` in pgcli code. Let's search memory of `pgcli/pgcompletion.py` from actual use. I have seen code:
```
for suggestion in suggestions:
    if suggestion['type'] == 'column':
        for table in suggestion['tables']:
            schema = table['schema']
            table_name = table['name']
            alias = table.get('alias')
```
I think this is from pgcli. So `tables` are dicts.

Let's search memory of `extract_tables` in `pgcli/packages/parseutils.py`. I can recall a function:
```
def extract_tables(sql):
    """Extract table names from a SQL statement."""
    tables = []
    for statement in sqlparse.parse(sql):
        for token in statement.tokens:
            if isinstance(token, Identifier):
                tables.append({'schema': token.get_schema(), 'name': token.get_real_name(), 'alias': token.get_alias()})
            elif isinstance(token, IdentifierList):
                for identifier in token.get_identifiers():
                    tables.append({'schema': identifier.get_schema(), 'name': identifier.get_real_name(), 'alias': identifier.get_alias()})
    return tables
```
This is simple and likely. So output dict with alias.

Now, schema None vs ''. Let's search memory of `Identifier.get_schema()` in sqlparse. It returns `None` if no schema. So schema None. If they wanted empty string, they would do `token.get_schema() or ''`. Did they? Let's search memory of `extract_tables` code. I can visualize:
```
tables.append({'schema': token.get_schema() or '',
               'name': token.get_real_name(),
               'alias': token.get_alias()})
```
Why would they use `or ''`? Because `None` schema might cause issues with `if schema:`? Both work. But in `PGCompleter`, when building qualified names, they might do:
```
if schema:
    qualified = schema + '.' + name
else:
    qualified = name
```
If schema is None, `if schema:` false. If schema is '', false. Both fine. So no need to convert. They likely leave None.

Let's search memory of pgcli tests. I recall a test:
```
def test_select_suggests_columns():
    suggestions = suggest_type('SELECT * FROM users', 'SELECT ')
    assert suggestions == [
        {'type': 'column', 'tables': [{'schema': None, 'name': 'users', 'alias': None}]},
        {'type': 'function', 'schema': []}
    ]
```
This seems very plausible. Let's go with that.

But wait, the input `'SELECT  FROM tabl'` has two spaces. `extract_tables` might parse `tabl` as an Identifier with `get_real_name()` returning `'tabl'`. Yes.

Let's also consider if `suggest_based_on_last_token` might return `[{'type': 'column', 'tables': []}, {'type': 'function', 'schema': []}]` because `extract_tables` only considers text before cursor? No, it receives full_text, not text_before_cursor. So it sees FROM tabl.

Let's verify by looking at the code flow: In `suggest_type`, if parsed len > 1, it adjusts full_text to current statement. Here parsed len=1, so full_text remains original. Then `suggest_based_on_last_token(last_token, text_before_cursor, full_text, identifier)` is called with full_text = 'SELECT  FROM tabl'. So yes.

Now, what about `text_before_cursor`? It is 'SELECT '. `suggest_based_on_last_token` might use it to determine if the cursor is inside a function call? Not for SELECT.

Thus final answer likely:
```
[{'type': 'column', 'tables': [{'schema': None, 'name': 'tabl', 'alias': None}]}, {'type': 'function', 'schema': []}]
```
or without alias:
```
[{'type': 'column', 'tables': [{'schema': None, 'name': 'tabl'}]}, {'type': 'function', 'schema': []}]
```
